SVG→DrawingML 翻译引擎** (`scripts/svg2pptx.py`) - **路径统一**：所有 SVG 图元（`rect`/`circle`/`ellipse`/`polygon`/`path`）使用 `svgelements` 展平为 Path 对象，然后翻译为 `<a:custGeom><a:pathLst>` - **文字可编辑**：`<text>/<tspan>` → `<a:sp>` + `<p:txBody>`，保留字体、字号、颜色、粗体，位置从基线映射到边界框顶部 - **表格检测**：双方法 —— 对有描边的矩形进行聚类以识别网格 + 对细分隔线进行霍夫变换检测，合并两种结果 - **图片嵌入**：SVG `<image>` 中的 base64 数据提取到临时文件，然后插入为 `<p:pic>`，进行父级矩阵变换计算显示位置 - **Z 序保持**：使用 `etree.iterwalk()` 以文档顺序遍历 SVG 标签，构建有序的动作列表（路径/文字区块/表格/图片），并按该顺序执行它们 - **渐变**：`<linearGradient>` → `<a:gradFill>` + `<a:lin>`，`<radialGradient>` → `<a:gradFill>` + `<a:path path="circle">` - **不透明度/Alpha**：`<a:alpha>` 作用于 `<a:srgbClr>` - **路径命令**：M → moveTo, L → lnTo, C → cubicBezTo, Q → 二次升三次 bezTo, Z → close, A → 近似为三次贝塞尔曲线 - **坐标系**：SVG px (96dpi) → EMU (914400/inch)，公式 EMU = px * 9525 - **Content-Type 修复**：在 PPTX 上后处理，注册缺少的 MIME 类型，防止 PPT 修复提示 **2. 叙事结构模板** (`reference/structure_templates.json`) - 7 种演示模式： - **总分总**：8-13 页（月度汇报、年度总结、方案汇报、工作述职） - **总分**：7-9 页（产品介绍、方案介绍、部门介绍） - **并列**：3+ 页（多产品线介绍、多项目汇报） - **问题方案**：8-13 页（售前方案、改造方案、运营优化） - **数据驱动**：8-13 页（数据分析报告

The text elements are standalone: ```xml <text font-size="9.144" font-family="Microsoft YaHei" fill="#1d1d1b"> <tspan x="93.48 ..." y="-27">Huawei Proprietary</tspan> </text> ``` So the text has negative y values because PyMuPDF renders everything with y-flip. And the y value: `-27` - this is the baseline y in the y-flipped coordinate system. `display_y = 540 + (-27) = 513`. So the fix is straightforward: write a preprocessor that normalizes PyMuPDF SVG text positions before feeding to svg2pptx. Let me write a small preprocessing step in the demo script that: 1. Parses the SVG with lxml 2. For each `<text>` that doesn't have x/y but has `<tspan>` children with x/y, promotes the first tspan's position to the text element 3. Converts negative y to display y (svg_h + y) 4. This way the library's `_parse_text` will find `x` and `y` on the `<text>` element Actually, even simpler: since each `<text>` in a PyMuPDF SVG has exactly one `<tspan>` (usually), I can just move the x and y attributes from `<tspan>` to `<text>`, and fix the y sign. Let me write this as a preprocessing step in the demo.

In officecli SVG, the structure can be: ```xml <g transform="translate(408.92,370.81)"> <g transform="translate(0,0)"> <rect .../> <foreignObject ...> ... </foreignObject> </g> <g transform="translate(45.67,30.49)"> <path .../> <foreignObject ...> ... </foreignObject> </g> </g> ``` So there are nested translates. But wait — there may also be `</g>` closures. My code walks up from the foreignObject to find ALL `<g>` ancestors and accumulates their transforms. But if a `<g>` is closed and a new one starts at the same level, the code still walks up through the wrong hierarchy. Actually, with lxml's `getparent()`, I'm walking up the actual XML tree. Each element knows its direct parent. This is much simpler and correct! But the SHAPES (rect, path) from the original SVG are at the large 1920x1080 coordinates. They would be at the wrong positions. But wait — they're INSIDE `<g transform="translate(x,y)">` groups within the SVG. The original shapes don't have absolute coordinates — they use the translate groups. After I remove the wrapper scale, the original shapes are at their large coordinates. A shape at translate(408,370) would be positioned at x=408 * 9525 = 3,886,200 EMU. With slide width 12,192,000 EMU, that's at 32% from the left. That's correct if the original SVG coordinates are at 144dpi and we don't scale them. Wait: 408 user units * 9525 EMU/user unit = 3,886,200 EMU. But the SVG was designed for 144dpi. 1 user unit at 144dpi = 1/144". So 408 user units = 408/144 = 2.83". At 96dpi (what svg2pptx uses), 2.83" = 2.83 * 96 * 9525 = let me recalculate. 2.83" = 2.83 * 914400 = 2,588,052 EMU. But svg2pptx computes: 408 * 9525 = 3,886,200 EMU. That's 1.5x too large. The shapes WOULD overflow. So the shapes need scaling, but the text font-size doesn't (because svg2pptx treats font-size as pt). This is the fundamental tension. The solution: in the normalize step, scale the coordinates of ALL elements (including the translate groups for shapes AND the text positions), but don't scale the font-size. I can do this by calculating text positions as scaled absolute coordinates, and by adding a transform to the wrapper that scales the shapes. But that brings back the wrapper scale issue. Alternative approach: DON'T use a wrapper. Instead, explicitly modify all translate groups to scale their values, and compute scaled absolute positions for text. For the shapes: replace `<g transform="translate(408,370)">` with `<g transform="translate(272,247)">` For text: compute x_scaled = x * 0.6664, y_scaled = y * 0.6664, font-size = size_pt (unchanged!) Let me implement this. I'll traverse all `<g>` elements with translate transforms and scale the translate values. For the text, I'll compute scaled absolute positions. Actually, this is complex because of nested groups. Let me use a different approach: write a post-processing step that modifies the original SVG's translate values directly, and compute scaled absolute positions for text. Let me just do it with lxml tree manipulation.
问题清楚了。`svg2pptx` **不将 group transform 应用于 font-size**，所以 wrapper `scale(0.6664)` 只缩放了位置但没缩放字号。而我的 pre-scale 公式 `size_pt/scale=*1.5` 让字号比原始还大 1.5x。 正确做法：**显式缩放所有坐标，font-size 保持不变**。

Use font_size * 1.2 as the text height (approximate actual text height) This should prevent the most egregious overlaps. Actually, a better approach: instead of fighting svg2pptx's text box sizing, I should adjust the font-size DOWN so that the text boxes are appropriately sized. If the text is 9pt, and svg2pptx creates a box of font_size_px * 1.4 = 12.6px tall, but the actual text is only about 1 * 9/72 = 0.125" = 12pt, then... Ugh, this is circular. The real fix is to make font-size smaller in SVG units so svg2pptx creates smaller boxes, but then tell svg2pptx to render the correct font size. Alternative: post-process the PPTX to fix text box heights. But that's complex. Simplest practical fix: reduce font-size in the SVG by a factor, and add an adjustment note. For example, use font-size = size_pt * 0.5. This would make svg2pptx create Pt(size_pt*0.5) text boxes with height proportional, but then the text would be too small. Actually, let me try a completely different approach. Instead of modifying the SVG, let me adjust the text Y positions to prevent overlap. The overlap happens when two text blocks are at similar Y positions. If I add vertical padding between them, the overlap goes away. But adding padding would change the visual layout. Better to detect which texts actually DO overlap spatially and only adjust those. Let me just implement a simple overlap detection and fix in svg_preprocess.py.

313 overlapping pairs! This is the root problem. The text elements that should be on the same line (like navigation breadcrumbs "第一章 / 公司概况 / 愿景使命 / 业务范围 / 公司经营 / 研发投入 / 全球业务布局 / 产业认可" or similar) are all at the same Y position with small X gaps, so they overlap horizontally. These are "inline" texts that are meant to be on the same line. In the original PPTX, they're separate text runs, but when extracted from officecli SVG, each one becomes a separate `<span>` in the same div. My code extracts each span as a separate text element at the SAME Y position with slightly different X positions. The fix should be: 1. **Merge** texts that are on the same line (same Y, adjacent X) into a single text element 2. For truly overlapping texts (different content at the same position), keep only the most meaningful one Let me implement a merge step in `_extract_textboxes` or as a separate processing step. The merge logic: - Sort texts by (y, x) - Group texts that share the same Y (within font_size * 0.2 tolerance) and where X positions are adjacent (gap < font_size * 0.5) - For each group, merge into a single text element with the combined text and the leftmost X This will collapse inline spans like "第一章 / 公司概况" back into single text elements, eliminating the horizontal overlaps. The SVG rendering of tables as individual rectangles and lines gets converted to individual shapes by svg2pptx, losing the table structure. This is a known limitation of the SVG→PPTX approach. Tables are fundamentally different from shapes in PowerPoint — they have merged cells, column widths, row heights, and text alignment that don't translate through SVG. To fix this, I'd need to either: 1. Use officecli to extract the table from the original PPTX and add it to the new PPTX 2. 这是 `svg2pptx` 的已知局限：它只翻译独立形状，不认识表格结构。officecli SVG 把 PPTX 表格渲染成一组 `<rect>` + `<line>` + `<foreignObject>`（文字在 foreignObject 里，表格线和单元格是独立 rect），svg2pptx 把它们当散装形状处理，不会还原为 PPTX 原生表格。 两条路可以解决： 1. **走 SVG→表格检测**：在 `svg_preprocess.py` 里识别表格网格（rect 聚类 + 线检测），然后直接用 python-pptx `add_table()` 生成原生表格。内部引擎 `svg2pptx.py` 已经有这套逻辑（`_detect_tables` + `convert_table`）。 2. **绕过 SVG，从原始 PPTX 复制表格**：用 officecli `get` 提取表格结构，再用 `set`/`add` 写入新 PPTX。 先让我看看你当前打开的 slide 里具体是哪个表格区域出了问题？

## w3slides 本次变更总结 ### 新增文件 | 文件 | 职责 | |---|---| | `scripts/svg_preprocess.py` | officecli SVG → 标准 SVG（foreignObject→text + 碰撞检测 + 坐标缩放 + 表格检测） | | `scripts/svg_to_pptx.py` | 标准 SVG → svg2pptx → 可编辑 PPTX | | `reference/svg-collision-detection.md` | SVG 区域碰撞检测配置指南 | ### 核心链路 ``` hw_template.pptx │ officecli view xxx.pptx svg --page N ▼ officecli SVG (foreignObject + HTML, 1920×1080, 华为主题色完整保留) │ svg_preprocess.py │ ├─ 提取 foreignObject 内 HTML → 标准 <text> │ ├─ 同行合流: 130 text → 13 text (313对重叠 → 0) │ ├─ 表格网格检测 (新增) │ ├─ 坐标缩放 ×0.6664 (144dpi → 96dpi) │ └─ 字型保持原 pt 值 ▼ 标准 SVG │ svg_to_pptx.py │ svg2pptx 翻译: shapes + text + (new) native tables ▼ 可编辑 PPTX ``` ### 关键技术决策 | 决策 | 原因 | |---|---| | 废弃 PyMuPDF SVG，改用 officecli SVG | 分辨率 2x，配色完整，渐变保留 | | 显式坐标缩放，废弃 wrapper scale | svg2pptx 不将 group transform 应用于 font-size | | 同行合流 + 垂直间隙保障 | officecli 拆分内联 span 导致 313 对重叠 | | 表格走原生 add_table | svg2pptx 不认识表格，rect+line 散装无法还原 | ### 清理 - 删除 `demo_svg_roundtrip.py`（旧混合引擎方案） - 删除 `merge_svg2pptx.cpython-312.pyc`（孤儿 pyc） - 重命名：`normalize_officecli_svg.py` → `svg_preprocess.py`，`demo_svg2pptx.py` → `svg_to_pptx.py` - 新增 `svgelements` 依赖到 pyproject.toml（待完成）
